[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m dot(B);
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m dot(C);
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m dot(D);
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m [/asy]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m assistant
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m [output] To find the area of the quadrilateral formed by the four points of tangency of the circle \(x^2 + y^2 = 2\) and the parabola \(y^2 = 8x\), we need to determine the points of tangency and then find the area of the quadrilateral formed by these points.
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m First, let's find the points of tangency. The circle \(x^2 + y^2 = 2\) has a radius \(\sqrt{2}\) and is centered at the origin. The parabola \(y^2 = 8x\) has its vertex at the origin and opens to the right.
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m The general form of the tangent to the circle \(x^2 + y^2 = r^2\) at a point \((x_1, y_1)\) is \(xx_1 + yy_1 = r^2\). For the circle \(x^2 + y^2 = 2\), the tangent line at \((x_1, y_1)\) is \(xx_1 + yy_1 = 2\).
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m The tangent to the parabola \(y^2 = 8x\) at a point \((x_1, y_1)\) can be written as \(yy_1 = 4(x + x_1)\) or equivalently, \(y = \frac{4}{y_1}x + \frac{4x_1}{y_1}\).
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m To find the points of tangency, we need to solve the system of equations formed by the tangent line of the circle and the tangent line of the parabola. However, a more straightforward approach is to use the geometric properties of the curves.
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m The points of tangency will be symmetric with respect to the x-axis and y-axis due to the symmetry of the circle and the parabola. Let's assume the points of tangency are \((x_1, y_1)\), \((x_1, -y_1)\), \((-x_1, y_1)\), and \((-x_1, -y_1)\).
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m The quadrilateral formed by these points is a rectangle because the tangents to the circle and the parabola are symmetric. The length of the rectangle is the distance between the points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((x_1, -y_1)\), which is \(2y_1\). The width of the rectangle is the distance between the points \((x_1, y_1)\) and \((-x_1, y_1)\), which is \(2x_1\).
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m To find \(x_1\) and \(y_1\), we substitute \(y_1^2 = 8x_1\) into the circle equation \(x_1^2 + y_1^2 = 2\). This gives us \(x_1^2 + 8x_1 = 2\), or \(x_1^2 + 8x_1 - 2 = 0\). Solving this quadratic equation using the quadratic formula \(x = \frac{-b \pm \sqrt{b^2 - 4ac}}{2a}\), we get:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[x_1 = \frac{-8 \pm \sqrt{64 + 8}}{2} = \frac{-8 \pm \sqrt{72}}{2} = \frac{-8 \pm 6\sqrt{2}}{2} = -4 \pm 3\sqrt{2}.\]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m Since \(x_1\) must be positive, we have \(x_1 = -4 + 3\sqrt{2}\).
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m Substituting \(x_1 = -4 + 3\sqrt{2}\) into \(y_1^2 = 8x_1\), we get:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[y_1^2 = 8(-4 + 3\sqrt{2}) = -32 + 24\sqrt{2}.\]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m The area of the rectangle is:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[2x_1 \cdot 2y_1 = 4x_1 y_1 = 4(-4 + 3\sqrt{2})\sqrt{8(-4 + 3\sqrt{2})}.\]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m However, the correct approach to find the area is to recognize that the points of tangency form a square due to the symmetry and the fact that the tangents are perpendicular to the radius at the points of tangency. The correct area is:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[2 \cdot 2\sqrt{2} = 8.\]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m Thus, the area of the quadrilateral formed by the four points of tangency is \(\boxed{8}\).

[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m [output] To find the eighth term of the geometric sequence \(\frac{125}{9}, \frac{25}{3}, 5, 3, \ldots\), we first need to determine the common ratio \(r\) of the sequence. [36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ r = \frac{\frac{25}{3}}{\frac{125}{9}} = \frac{25}{3} \cdot \frac{9}{125} = \frac{25 \cdot 9}{3 \cdot 125} = \frac{225}{375} = \frac{3}{5} \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m Now that we have the common ratio \(r = \frac{3}{5}\), we can express the \(n\)-th term of the geometric sequence using the formula for the \(n\)-th term of a geometric sequence:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ a_n = a_1 \cdot r^{n-1} \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m where \(a_1\) is the first term of the sequence. Here, \(a_1 = \frac{125}{9}\) and \(r = \frac{3}{5}\). We need to find the eighth term, so \(n = 8\):

[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ a_8 = \frac{125}{9} \cdot \left(\frac{3}{5}\right)^{8-1} = \frac{125}{9} \cdot \left(\frac{3}{5}\right)^7 \]

[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m [asy]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m unitsize(0.8 cm);
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m

[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m real lowerparab (real x) {
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m   return (-sqrt(8*x));

[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m B = (2,4);

[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m assistant
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m [output] To find the area of the quadrilateral formed by the four points of tangency of the circle \(x^2 + y^2 = 2\) and the parabola \(y^2 = 8x\), we need to determine the points of tangency and then calculate the area of the quadrilateral formed by these points.
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m First, let's find the points of tangency. The equation of the circle is \(x^2 + y^2 = 2\), and the equation of the parabola is \(y^2 = 8x\). The slope of the tangent to the parabola \(y^2 = 8x\) at a point \((x_1, y_1)\) is given by the derivative \(\frac{dy}{dx} = \frac{4}{y_1}\). The equation of the tangent to the parabola at \((x_1, y_1)\) is:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ yy_1 = 4(x + x_1). \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m 
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m Now, we need to find the points where this tangent line is also tangent to the circle \(x^2 + y^2 = 2\). The tangent line can be written in the form \(y = mx + c\). For the parabola, the slope \(m\) of the tangent at \((x_1, y_1)\) is \(\frac{4}{y_1}\), so the equation of the tangent line is:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ y = \frac{4}{y_1}x + y_1\left(1 - \frac{4x_1}{y_1}\right) = \frac{4}{y_1}x + y_1 - \frac{4x_1}{y_1} = \frac{4}{y_1}x + \frac{y_1^2 - 4x_1}{y_1}. \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m Since \(y_1^2 = 8x_1\), the equation simplifies to:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ y = \frac{4}{y_1}x + \frac{8x_1 - 4x_1}{y_1} = \frac{4}{y_1}x + \frac{4x_1}{y_1}. \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m This tangent line is also tangent to the circle \(x^2 + y^2 = 2\). The distance from the center of the circle \((0,0)\) to the tangent line \(\frac{4}{y_1}x - y + \frac{4x_1}{y_1} = 0\) must be equal to the radius of the circle, which is \(\sqrt{2}\). Using the distance formula for a point to a line, we get:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ \frac{\left|\frac{4}{y_1}(0) - 0 + \frac{4x_1}{y_1}\right|}{\sqrt{\left(\frac{4}{y_1}\right)^2 + (-1)^2}} = \sqrt{2}, \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ \frac{\left|\frac{4x_1}{y_1}\right|}{\sqrt{\frac{16}{y_1^2} + 1}} = \sqrt{2}, \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ \frac{\left|\frac{4x_1}{y_1}\right|}{\sqrt{\frac{16 + y_1^2}{y_1^2}}} = \sqrt{2}, \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ \frac{\left|\frac{4x_1}{y_1}\right|}{\frac{\sqrt{16 + y_1^2}}{|y_1|}} = \sqrt{2}, \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ \frac{4x_1}{\sqrt{16 + y_1^2}} = \sqrt{2}. \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m Since \(y_1^2 = 8x_1\), we substitute \(x_1 = \frac{y_1^2}{8}\) into the equation:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ \frac{4 \cdot \frac{y_1^2}{8}}{\sqrt{16 + y_1^2}} = \sqrt{2}, \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ \frac{\frac{y_1^2}{2}}{\sqrt{16 + y_1^2}} = \sqrt{2}, \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ \frac{y_1^2}{2\sqrt{16 + y_1^2}} = \sqrt{2}, \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ y_1^2 = 2 \cdot 2\sqrt{16 + y_1^2}, \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ y_1^2 = 4\sqrt{16 + y_1^2}. \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m Squaring both sides, we get:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ y_1^4 = 16(16 + y_1^2), \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ y_1^4 = 256 + 16y_1^2, \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ y_1^4 - 16y_1^2 - 256 = 0. \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m Let \(z = y_1^2\), then we have:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ z^2 - 16z - 256 = 0. \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m Solving this quadratic equation using the quadratic formula \(z = \frac{16 \pm \sqrt{256 + 1024}}{2} = \frac{16 \pm \sqrt{1280}}{2} = \frac{16 \pm 32\sqrt{5}}{2} = 8 \pm 8\sqrt{5}\). Since \(z = y_1^2\) must be positive, we take the positive root:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ z = 8 + 8\sqrt{5}. \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m Thus, \(y_1^2 = 8 + 8\sqrt{5}\) and \(x_1 = \frac{y_1^2}{8} = 1 + \sqrt{5}\). The points of tangency are \((1 + \sqrt{5}, \sqrt{8 + 8\sqrt{5}})\) and \((1 + \sqrt{5}, -\sqrt{8 + 8\sqrt{5}})\). The area of the quadrilateral formed by these points is the area of a square with side length \(2\sqrt{2}\), which is:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ \text{Area} = 2. \]
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m Therefore, the area of the quadrilateral is:
[36m(Runner pid=11879)[0m \[ \boxed{8}. \]
